Um, hi. I guess I'm here because... well, for the last couple of years, I just feel really low all the time. It's like this heavy, empty feeling that doesn't ever really lift. Even when I'm at work... I work as an inspector, mostly weighing and sorting these little parts all day on an assembly line. 

Actually, the warehouse where I sort things is just freezing right now. The heater broke like three weeks ago, and my boss keeps saying he'll call someone to fix it, but he never does. I had to buy these special fingerless gloves online just so I could still feel the buttons on the digital scale without my hands going numb. My mom said I should just complain to HR about it, but HR is basically just my boss's sister, so that wouldn't do anything anyway. She always takes his side on everything. 

But... yeah, even when I'm at work doing that, or at church, or just around people I actually care about... that heavy feeling is just always there.

Yeah, um, all the time. It’s like... even the really small stuff just takes more energy than I have. Like washing my hair. I actually just bought this new shampoo, it was on sale at Target and it’s supposed to smell like coconut and mango, but it honestly just smells like burnt plastic? My landlord is honestly just as bad as my boss with the broken heater, he never fixes anything. 

But yeah, washing my hair is just exhausting. And at work, just filling out the inspection logs drains me. We have to use these specific blue pens for the logs, and they always run out of ink halfway through the shift, and if you try to use a black pen, HR throws a fit about company policy. It's so stupid. But just doing that, or like, driving to basketball... which is a whole other thing because my car makes this weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. My mom thinks it's the transmission because she watched a YouTube video about it, but she doesn't actually know anything about cars. 

But anyway, yeah, I just don't have the energy for any of it. And even when I just lay around and rest on my days off, it doesn't really refill me. I'm just always running on empty.

Um, yeah, I guess that's really why I'm here. For the last couple of years, honestly, I just feel low all the time. Like, nearly every day. It's just this heavy, empty feeling that doesn't really lift, even when I'm at work or at church, or around people I care about. Like my friend Sarah from church, she's always trying to get me to join the choir. She sings soprano and takes it way too seriously. She bought this ridiculous velvet dress for the Christmas cantata last year, and it was so long she kept tripping over it on the risers. I told her she should get it hemmed, but she said the tailor down the street charges too much. Which is true, I went to him once to get a zipper fixed on my favorite jacket, and he wanted like forty dollars. I could just buy a new jacket for that price, you know? There's this boutique downtown that has really cute stuff, but parking there is a nightmare. They only have street parking, and the meters only take quarters. Who even carries quarters anymore? I used to keep a jar of them in my car for the car wash, but then the car wash switched to a card reader, and the card reader is always broken anyway. It's the one by the gas station where the guy behind the counter is always listening to really loud talk radio. But um... yeah, to answer your question, it's just been this constant low feeling for a really long time now.

I tried soaking it in OxiClean for like two days, but it just won't come out. It's so annoying. But, um... no, I don't really feel like I'm not good enough or anything like that. I just... Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.

I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. Most days I honestly just skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing really sounds good. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.

They're so aggressive, one of them literally chased my cousin's little boy for like half a mile just because he had a hotdog bun. The hotdogs weren't even that good, my uncle bought the cheap brand from Aldi instead of the all-beef ones. They get all weird and rubbery when you grill them. And the mustard bottle exploded on my favorite sweater... it's this really soft, oversized gray one I got from a thrift store, and now it has this huge yellow stain right on the front. I tried soaking it in OxiClean for like two days, but it just won't come out. It's so annoying. But, um... no, I don't really feel like I'm not good enough or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just feel empty, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or that you’re failing others, even when things aren’t really your fault?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, not really? I don't think I feel like I'm failing others or anything. Like, I do my job, even though the inspection logs are a nightmare. Honestly, the worst part of my job isn't even the logs, it's the break room. They have this vending machine that is supposed to be stocked every Tuesday, but the guy who does it is always late. And when he finally does show up, he fills like three whole rows with those weird spicy pork rinds. Nobody eats them! I watched one bag sit there for like three months until the expiration date passed, and he still didn't take it out. I wanted to complain to management, but our supervisor is always on maternity leave. It's like she has a baby every single year. She brought the newest one in last week, and he had this tiny little hat with bear ears on it. It was kind of cute, I guess, but babies always smell like sour milk to me. And the noise... he just cried the whole time she was showing him off. I don't understand how people deal with that all day. My cat is way better, even though she keeps throwing up on my rug. It's this cheap rug from IKEA, but still, it's annoying to clean. But, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, about failing people. No, I don't really feel like I'm not worth much, I just... I just don't feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's really why I'm here. For the last couple of years, honestly, I just feel low all the time. Like, nearly every day. It's just this heavy, empty feeling that doesn't really lift, even when I'm at work or at church, or around people I care about. Like my friend Sarah from church, she's always trying to get me to join the choir. She sings soprano and takes it way too seriously. She bought this ridiculous velvet dress for the Christmas cantata last year, and it was so long she kept tripping over it on the risers. I told her she should get it hemmed, but she said the tailor down the street charges too much. Which is true, I went to him once to get a zipper fixed on my favorite jacket, and he wanted like forty dollars. I could just buy a new jacket for that price, you know? There's this boutique downtown that has really cute stuff, but parking there is a nightmare. They only have street parking, and the meters only take quarters. Who even carries quarters anymore? I used to keep a jar of them in my car for the car wash, but then the car wash switched to a card reader, and the card reader is always broken anyway. It's the one by the gas station where the guy behind the counter is always listening to really loud talk radio. But um... yeah, to answer your question, it's just been this constant low feeling for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, have you been more restless or fidgety than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving slow or, you know, being fidgety. My sister is the fidgety one. She literally cannot sit still for a movie. We tried to watch that new superhero movie last weekend, the one that's like three hours long? And she kept getting up to go to the kitchen, or checking her phone, or playing with her cuticles. She always picks at her cuticles until they bleed, which is so gross. I keep telling her to buy this special cuticle oil I found online. It smells like lavender and vanilla, which is way better than the cheap lotion she uses. Her lotion smells like fake coconut, it reminds me of those little tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have a pine-scented one in his old truck, and it was so strong it gave me a headache every time we drove to the grocery store. He refused to shop anywhere but the Piggly Wiggly because he liked the guy in the meat department. But, um... no, I don't really feel restless or slow or anything. Just the same old empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt guilty about things you didn't do or felt like you were a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. Most days I honestly just skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing really sounds good. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. It's just been there every day.

And the pump on the bottle is broken so I have to unscrew the top every single time. My apartment's water pressure is awful too, so it takes forever to rinse anything out. We have to use these specific blue pens for the logs, and they always run out of ink halfway through the shift, and if you try to use a black pen, HR throws a fit about company policy. It's so stupid. But just doing that, or like, driving to basketball... which is a whole other thing because my car makes this weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. My mom thinks it's the transmission because she watched a YouTube video about it, but she doesn't actually know anything about cars. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just don't have the energy for any of it. And even when I just lay around and rest on my days off, it doesn't really refill me. I'm just always running on empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things that aren't your fault?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know... I guess I haven't really noticed feeling like that. Like, I don't think I blame myself for things that aren't my fault. My mom does that, though. Like, she'll blame herself if the weather is bad when we have a family picnic planned, which is just crazy because she can't control the rain. We actually went to this park last month, the one by the lake with the duck pond? And there were all these geese everywhere. They're so aggressive, one of them literally chased my cousin's little boy for like half a mile just because he had a hotdog bun. The hotdogs weren't even that good, my uncle bought the cheap brand from Aldi instead of the all-beef ones. They get all weird and rubbery when you grill them. And the mustard bottle exploded on my favorite sweater... it's this really soft, oversized gray one I got from a thrift store, and now it has this huge yellow stain right on the front. I tried soaking it in OxiClean for like two days, but it just won't come out. It's so annoying. But, um... no, I don't really feel like I'm not good enough or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just feel empty, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or that you’re failing others, even when things aren’t really your fault?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, not really? I don't think I feel like I'm failing others or anything. Like, I do my job, even though the inspection logs are a nightmare. Honestly, the worst part of my job isn't even the logs, it's the break room. They have this vending machine that is supposed to be stocked every Tuesday, but the guy who does it is always late. And when he finally does show up, he fills like three whole rows with those weird spicy pork rinds. Nobody eats them! I watched one bag sit there for like three months until the expiration date passed, and he still didn't take it out. I wanted to complain to management, but our supervisor is always on maternity leave. It's like she has a baby every single year. She brought the newest one in last week, and he had this tiny little hat with bear ears on it. It was kind of cute, I guess, but babies always smell like sour milk to me. And the noise... he just cried the whole time she was showing him off. I don't understand how people deal with that all day. My cat is way better, even though she keeps throwing up on my rug. It's this cheap rug from IKEA, but still, it's annoying to clean. But, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, about failing people. No, I don't really feel like I'm not worth much, I just... I just don't feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's really why I'm here. For the last couple of years, honestly, I just feel low all the time. Like, nearly every day. It's just this heavy, empty feeling that doesn't really lift, even when I'm at work or at church, or around people I care about. Like my friend Sarah from church, she's always trying to get me to join the choir. She sings soprano and takes it way too seriously. She bought this ridiculous velvet dress for the Christmas cantata last year, and it was so long she kept tripping over it on the risers. I told her she should get it hemmed, but she said the tailor down the street charges too much. Which is true, I went to him once to get a zipper fixed on my favorite jacket, and he wanted like forty dollars. I could just buy a new jacket for that price, you know? There's this boutique downtown that has really cute stuff, but parking there is a nightmare. They only have street parking, and the meters only take quarters. Who even carries quarters anymore? I used to keep a jar of them in my car for the car wash, but then the car wash switched to a card reader, and the card reader is always broken anyway. It's the one by the gas station where the guy behind the counter is always listening to really loud talk radio. But um... yeah, to answer your question, it's just been this constant low feeling for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, have you been more restless or fidgety than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving slow or, you know, being fidgety. My sister is the fidgety one. She literally cannot sit still for a movie. We tried to watch that new superhero movie last weekend, the one that's like three hours long? And she kept getting up to go to the kitchen, or checking her phone, or playing with her cuticles. She always picks at her cuticles until they bleed, which is so gross. I keep telling her to buy this special cuticle oil I found online. It smells like lavender and vanilla, which is way better than the cheap lotion she uses. Her lotion smells like fake coconut, it reminds me of those little tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have a pine-scented one in his old truck, and it was so strong it gave me a headache every time we drove to the grocery store. He refused to shop anywhere but the Piggly Wiggly because he liked the guy in the meat department. But, um... no, I don't really feel restless or slow or anything. Just the same old empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt guilty about things you didn't do or felt like you were a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. Most days I honestly just skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing really sounds good. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling low for a couple of years — has there been any period of time in the last two years when you felt this low mood for most of the day, more days than not, without a break lasting at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. It's just been there every day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It's just been there every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that your movements or speech slowed down, or that you couldn’t sit still and felt restless, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've noticed anything like that. I mean, I sit pretty still at work, mostly because I have to. They give us these really uncomfortable metal stools on the line to sit on while we inspect the parts, and they don't have any padding at all. My supervisor, Carol, she has this fancy ergonomic mesh chair in her office that squeaks really loud every time she leans back. It sounds exactly like this rubber chicken my little cousin used to have. He brought it to Thanksgiving dinner a couple of years ago and kept squeezing it during the prayer, and my uncle got so mad he actually threw it over the fence into the neighbor's yard. The neighbors had this huge Rottweiler named Buster who completely shredded it in like two seconds. There was just yellow rubber everywhere in their grass. It snowed the next day, so you could just see these little bright yellow pieces sticking out of the white snow all winter. I always felt kind of bad for the dog, like what if he swallowed a piece? But um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, moving slow or being restless. No, I don't really get like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nLooking back, would you say there have been times when you completely lost interest in things you used to care about, like hobbies, socializing, or even things you looked forward to?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / inspector_tester_sorter_sampler_or_weigher / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"I’ve gotten written warnings at work because I’m missing shifts and turning in inspection documentation late, and I’ve mostly stopped showing up for basketball, dance nights, and the church STEM mentoring I used to care about.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, actually. I used to do a lot more. Like, I'd go play pickup basketball at the rec center, or go to these dance nights with some friends, and I even did this STEM mentoring thing at my church for the middle schoolers. But I just... I mostly stopped showing up for all of it. I don't even have the energy for work anymore, honestly. I've gotten written warnings lately because I keep missing shifts, and when I am there, I'm turning in my inspection documentation late. The paperwork is just so tedious. It's all these tiny little boxes you have to check with a black pen—they get really mad if you use blue ink. My supervisor, Carol, the one with the squeaky chair? She sent out this whole memo about only using black ink because the scanner doesn't pick up blue well enough. Which is weird, because my scanner at home picks up everything. I bought it at a garage sale for like five bucks from this guy who was moving to Florida. He had all these crazy Hawaiian shirts he was trying to sell, too. Like, neon pink with bright green palm trees. I almost bought one just as a joke, but I didn't have enough cash on me. I only had a twenty and he didn't have change. But um, yeah, I just don't care about the stuff I used to do anymore.

It smells like lavender and vanilla, which is way better than the cheap lotion she uses. My dad used to have a pine-scented one in his old truck, and it was so strong it gave me a headache every time we drove to the grocery store. He refused to shop anywhere but the Piggly Wiggly because he liked the guy in the meat department. But, um... no, I don't really feel restless or slow or anything. Just the same old empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt guilty about things you didn't do or felt like you were a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling low for a couple of years — has there been any period of time in the last two years when you felt this low mood for most of the day, more days than not, without a break lasting at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. Like my friend Sarah from church, she's always trying to get me to join the choir. She sings soprano and takes it way too seriously. She bought this ridiculous velvet dress for the Christmas cantata last year, and it was so long she kept tripping over it on the risers. I told her she should get it hemmed, but she said the tailor down the street charges too much. Which is true, I went to him once to get a zipper fixed on my favorite jacket, and he wanted like forty dollars. I could just buy a new jacket for that price, you know? There's this boutique downtown that has really cute stuff, but parking there is a nightmare. They only have street parking, and the meters only take quarters. Who even carries quarters anymore? I used to keep a jar of them in my car for the car wash, but then the car wash switched to a card reader, and the card reader is always broken anyway. It's the one by the gas station where the guy behind the counter is always listening to really loud talk radio. But um... yeah, to answer your question, it's just been this constant low feeling for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, have you been more restless or fidgety than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving slow or, you know, being fidgety. My sister is the fidgety one. She literally cannot sit still for a movie. We tried to watch that new superhero movie last weekend, the one that's like three hours long? And she kept getting up to go to the kitchen, or checking her phone, or playing with her cuticles. She always picks at her cuticles until they bleed, which is so gross. I keep telling her to buy this special cuticle oil I found online. It smells like lavender and vanilla, which is way better than the cheap lotion she uses. Her lotion smells like fake coconut, it reminds me of those little tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have a pine-scented one in his old truck, and it was so strong it gave me a headache every time we drove to the grocery store. He refused to shop anywhere but the Piggly Wiggly because he liked the guy in the meat department. But, um... no, I don't really feel restless or slow or anything. Just the same old empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt guilty about things you didn't do or felt like you were a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. Most days I honestly just skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing really sounds good. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling low for a couple of years — has there been any period of time in the last two years when you felt this low mood for most of the day, more days than not, without a break lasting at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. It's just been there every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that your movements or speech slowed down, or that you couldn’t sit still and felt restless, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've noticed anything like that. I mean, I sit pretty still at work, mostly because I have to. They give us these really uncomfortable metal stools on the line to sit on while we inspect the parts, and they don't have any padding at all. My supervisor, Carol, she has this fancy ergonomic mesh chair in her office that squeaks really loud every time she leans back. It sounds exactly like this rubber chicken my little cousin used to have. He brought it to Thanksgiving dinner a couple of years ago and kept squeezing it during the prayer, and my uncle got so mad he actually threw it over the fence into the neighbor's yard. The neighbors had this huge Rottweiler named Buster who completely shredded it in like two seconds. There was just yellow rubber everywhere in their grass. It snowed the next day, so you could just see these little bright yellow pieces sticking out of the white snow all winter. I always felt kind of bad for the dog, like what if he swallowed a piece? But um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, moving slow or being restless. No, I don't really get like that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there have been times when you completely lost interest in things you used to care about, like hobbies, socializing, or even things you looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually. I used to do a lot more. Like, I'd go play pickup basketball at the rec center, or go to these dance nights with some friends, and I even did this STEM mentoring thing at my church for the middle schoolers. But I just... I mostly stopped showing up for all of it. I don't even have the energy for work anymore, honestly. I've gotten written warnings lately because I keep missing shifts, and when I am there, I'm turning in my inspection documentation late. The paperwork is just so tedious. It's all these tiny little boxes you have to check with a black pen—they get really mad if you use blue ink. My supervisor, Carol, the one with the squeaky chair? She sent out this whole memo about only using black ink because the scanner doesn't pick up blue well enough. Which is weird, because my scanner at home picks up everything. I bought it at a garage sale for like five bucks from this guy who was moving to Florida. He had all these crazy Hawaiian shirts he was trying to sell, too. Like, neon pink with bright green palm trees. I almost bought one just as a joke, but I didn't have enough cash on me. I only had a twenty and he didn't have change. But um, yeah, I just don't care about the stuff I used to do anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, no, I don't think so. Like, before these last couple of years, I never really felt like this for that long. I mean, I'd have a bad day here and there, like when my car broke down on the highway. It was my old Honda, and the radiator just completely gave out right in the middle of rush hour traffic. Smoke was everywhere, and it smelled so bad, like burning syrup. He said the iguana liked the heat from the engine. It was wearing a tiny little sweater, which I didn't even know they made for lizards. He told me he knits them himself while he's waiting for calls. He actually offered to make me a scarf, but I never heard from him again. Probably for the best, honestly, I'm allergic to wool. It makes my neck break out in these itchy red patches that take forever to go away. I tried wearing this wool turtleneck for family pictures once, and I looked miserable in every single photo. My mom still has them hanging in the hallway, right next to the thermostat that doesn't work. But, um... no, I didn't have any long stretches of feeling down before all this started.

The music there used to make me feel something, but lately, I just sit there and look at the stained glass windows. There's this one window on the left side, it's supposed to be Noah's Ark, I think, but the animals look so weird. The giraffe has this incredibly short neck, and the lions look more like golden retrievers. I keep meaning to ask Pastor Dave who designed it, because it just looks so rushed. My mom thinks it's beautiful, but she also thinks my boss is a good person just because he gave us a turkey at Thanksgiving. A frozen turkey, which took forever to thaw, and honestly, it was so dry when we finally cooked it. But... what were you asking? Oh, right, activities. Yeah, I'm not really sure if I've lost interest, it's more like I just don't have the energy to care either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fatigued or like you don’t have enough energy to get through the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um, all the time. It’s like... even the really small stuff just takes more energy than I have. Like washing my hair. I actually just bought this new shampoo, it was on sale at Target and it’s supposed to smell like coconut and mango, but it honestly just smells like burnt plastic? And the pump on the bottle is broken so I have to unscrew the top every single time. My apartment's water pressure is awful too, so it takes forever to rinse anything out. My landlord is honestly just as bad as my boss with the broken heater, he never fixes anything. \n\nBut yeah, washing my hair is just exhausting. And at work, just filling out the inspection logs drains me. We have to use these specific blue pens for the logs, and they always run out of ink halfway through the shift, and if you try to use a black pen, HR throws a fit about company policy. It's so stupid. But just doing that, or like, driving to basketball... which is a whole other thing because my car makes this weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. My mom thinks it's the transmission because she watched a YouTube video about it, but she doesn't actually know anything about cars. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just don't have the energy for any of it. And even when I just lay around and rest on my days off, it doesn't really refill me. I'm just always running on empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things that aren't your fault?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know... I guess I haven't really noticed feeling like that. Like, I don't think I blame myself for things that aren't my fault. My mom does that, though. Like, she'll blame herself if the weather is bad when we have a family picnic planned, which is just crazy because she can't control the rain. We actually went to this park last month, the one by the lake with the duck pond? And there were all these geese everywhere. They're so aggressive, one of them literally chased my cousin's little boy for like half a mile just because he had a hotdog bun. The hotdogs weren't even that good, my uncle bought the cheap brand from Aldi instead of the all-beef ones. They get all weird and rubbery when you grill them. And the mustard bottle exploded on my favorite sweater... it's this really soft, oversized gray one I got from a thrift store, and now it has this huge yellow stain right on the front. I tried soaking it in OxiClean for like two days, but it just won't come out. It's so annoying. But, um... no, I don't really feel like I'm not good enough or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just feel empty, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or that you’re failing others, even when things aren’t really your fault?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, not really? I don't think I feel like I'm failing others or anything. Like, I do my job, even though the inspection logs are a nightmare. Honestly, the worst part of my job isn't even the logs, it's the break room. They have this vending machine that is supposed to be stocked every Tuesday, but the guy who does it is always late. And when he finally does show up, he fills like three whole rows with those weird spicy pork rinds. Nobody eats them! I watched one bag sit there for like three months until the expiration date passed, and he still didn't take it out. I wanted to complain to management, but our supervisor is always on maternity leave. It's like she has a baby every single year. She brought the newest one in last week, and he had this tiny little hat with bear ears on it. It was kind of cute, I guess, but babies always smell like sour milk to me. And the noise... he just cried the whole time she was showing him off. I don't understand how people deal with that all day. My cat is way better, even though she keeps throwing up on my rug. It's this cheap rug from IKEA, but still, it's annoying to clean. But, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, about failing people. No, I don't really feel like I'm not worth much, I just... I just don't feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess that's really why I'm here. For the last couple of years, honestly, I just feel low all the time. Like, nearly every day. It's just this heavy, empty feeling that doesn't really lift, even when I'm at work or at church, or around people I care about. Like my friend Sarah from church, she's always trying to get me to join the choir. She sings soprano and takes it way too seriously. She bought this ridiculous velvet dress for the Christmas cantata last year, and it was so long she kept tripping over it on the risers. I told her she should get it hemmed, but she said the tailor down the street charges too much. Which is true, I went to him once to get a zipper fixed on my favorite jacket, and he wanted like forty dollars. I could just buy a new jacket for that price, you know? There's this boutique downtown that has really cute stuff, but parking there is a nightmare. They only have street parking, and the meters only take quarters. Who even carries quarters anymore? I used to keep a jar of them in my car for the car wash, but then the car wash switched to a card reader, and the card reader is always broken anyway. It's the one by the gas station where the guy behind the counter is always listening to really loud talk radio. But um... yeah, to answer your question, it's just been this constant low feeling for a really long time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, have you been more restless or fidgety than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving slow or, you know, being fidgety. My sister is the fidgety one. She literally cannot sit still for a movie. We tried to watch that new superhero movie last weekend, the one that's like three hours long? And she kept getting up to go to the kitchen, or checking her phone, or playing with her cuticles. She always picks at her cuticles until they bleed, which is so gross. I keep telling her to buy this special cuticle oil I found online. It smells like lavender and vanilla, which is way better than the cheap lotion she uses. Her lotion smells like fake coconut, it reminds me of those little tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have a pine-scented one in his old truck, and it was so strong it gave me a headache every time we drove to the grocery store. He refused to shop anywhere but the Piggly Wiggly because he liked the guy in the meat department. But, um... no, I don't really feel restless or slow or anything. Just the same old empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt guilty about things you didn't do or felt like you were a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I feel guilty or like a burden. I mean, I do my own thing mostly. My roommate, though, she definitely makes me feel like a burden sometimes, even when I haven't done anything. Like, she gets so mad if I leave a single coffee mug in the sink. And it's not even a big deal, because I always wash it before I go to bed. But she acts like I've ruined her whole day. She bought this really expensive espresso machine last month, the kind that grinds the beans right there, and it's so loud it wakes me up at like 6 AM every morning. I tried to ask her to wait until 7, but she said she needs her caffeine before her yoga class. Which is stupid, because who drinks espresso right before downward dog? I went to a yoga class once, at the community center, and the instructor kept playing this weird flute music that sounded like a dying bird. Plus, the mats smelled like old gym socks. I ended up leaving halfway through and getting a smoothie instead. The smoothie place was out of strawberries, so I had to get mango, which was fine, but it gave me a brain freeze. But um, yeah... no, I don't really feel guilty about things. Just, you know, just kind of numb mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. Most days I honestly just skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing really sounds good. I work as an inspector, checking parts on this assembly line, and the break room always smells like burnt popcorn anyway. The guy who works the shift before me, Dave, he literally burns a bag of popcorn every single afternoon. I don't understand how someone can eat burnt popcorn, it smells like a chemical fire. He uses the microwave by the vending machines, which is where I end up grabbing snacks late in my shift just because I'm so drained. Usually it's just like, a bag of chips or those little powdered donuts. The machine used to have these really good peanut butter crackers, but they replaced them with some weird organic granola bars that taste like sawdust. I tried one once and it got stuck in my teeth for hours. I had to use a paperclip to get it out because I forgot my floss. My dentist is always yelling at me about flossing. He has this huge fish tank in his waiting room, though, which is kind of cool. There's this one orange fish that just stares at you while you read magazines. But, um, yeah, because of the weird eating, my weight has been going up and down a lot. Like, my work pants fit totally different from month to month, which is annoying because they make us buy our own uniforms and they're really expensive.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really think about ending it or anything. Sometimes it just feels like every day is exactly the same, you know? Just waking up and going to the assembly line. My car has been making this weird rattling noise every time I start it in the mornings, too. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around inside the engine. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment, but he said he couldn't hear anything wrong with it. Which is crazy, because it's so loud. His shop was full of these stray cats, though. He leaves bowls of food out for them everywhere. There was this one little black cat with a white paw that kept rubbing against my leg while I was trying to explain the car noise. I wanted to take him home, but my lease says absolutely no pets. The landlord is super strict about it. He even tried to evict the girl in 3B because she had a hamster. Like, how much damage can a hamster really do? They just run on those little wheels all night. I had a hamster when I was little, named Mr. Whiskers, and he escaped his cage once and we found him three days later sleeping in my mom's winter boot. But um... yeah, no, I don't have thoughts like that about ending things. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling low for a couple of years — has there been any period of time in the last two years when you felt this low mood for most of the day, more days than not, without a break lasting at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. It's just been there every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that your movements or speech slowed down, or that you couldn’t sit still and felt restless, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've noticed anything like that. I mean, I sit pretty still at work, mostly because I have to. They give us these really uncomfortable metal stools on the line to sit on while we inspect the parts, and they don't have any padding at all. My supervisor, Carol, she has this fancy ergonomic mesh chair in her office that squeaks really loud every time she leans back. It sounds exactly like this rubber chicken my little cousin used to have. He brought it to Thanksgiving dinner a couple of years ago and kept squeezing it during the prayer, and my uncle got so mad he actually threw it over the fence into the neighbor's yard. The neighbors had this huge Rottweiler named Buster who completely shredded it in like two seconds. There was just yellow rubber everywhere in their grass. It snowed the next day, so you could just see these little bright yellow pieces sticking out of the white snow all winter. I always felt kind of bad for the dog, like what if he swallowed a piece? But um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, moving slow or being restless. No, I don't really get like that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say there have been times when you completely lost interest in things you used to care about, like hobbies, socializing, or even things you looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually. I used to do a lot more. Like, I'd go play pickup basketball at the rec center, or go to these dance nights with some friends, and I even did this STEM mentoring thing at my church for the middle schoolers. But I just... I mostly stopped showing up for all of it. I don't even have the energy for work anymore, honestly. I've gotten written warnings lately because I keep missing shifts, and when I am there, I'm turning in my inspection documentation late. The paperwork is just so tedious. It's all these tiny little boxes you have to check with a black pen—they get really mad if you use blue ink. My supervisor, Carol, the one with the squeaky chair? She sent out this whole memo about only using black ink because the scanner doesn't pick up blue well enough. Which is weird, because my scanner at home picks up everything. I bought it at a garage sale for like five bucks from this guy who was moving to Florida. He had all these crazy Hawaiian shirts he was trying to sell, too. Like, neon pink with bright green palm trees. I almost bought one just as a joke, but I didn't have enough cash on me. I only had a twenty and he didn't have change. But um, yeah, I just don't care about the stuff I used to do anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time before this current two-year stretch where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. Like, before these last couple of years, I never really felt like this for that long. I mean, I'd have a bad day here and there, like when my car broke down on the highway. It was my old Honda, and the radiator just completely gave out right in the middle of rush hour traffic. Smoke was everywhere, and it smelled so bad, like burning syrup. I had to wait for a tow truck for three hours on the side of the road in the freezing cold. The tow truck driver was this guy named Earl who had a pet iguana sitting on his dashboard. He said the iguana liked the heat from the engine. It was wearing a tiny little sweater, which I didn't even know they made for lizards. He told me he knits them himself while he's waiting for calls. He actually offered to make me a scarf, but I never heard from him again. Probably for the best, honestly, I'm allergic to wool. It makes my neck break out in these itchy red patches that take forever to go away. I tried wearing this wool turtleneck for family pictures once, and I looked miserable in every single photo. My mom still has them hanging in the hallway, right next to the thermostat that doesn't work. But, um... no, I didn't have any long stretches of feeling down before all this started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Just... just tired of how empty everything feels.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling low for a couple of years — has there been any period of time in the last two years when you felt this low mood for most of the day, more days than not, without a break lasting at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, it's been pretty constant. Like, I haven't really had a break from it for... well, it's been about two years and four months now. It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule at the plant was getting so crazy. They kept changing my shifts, making me work these split shifts where I'd have like four hours off in the middle of the day. What are you even supposed to do with four hours? It's not enough time to go home and actually relax, but it's too long to just sit in the break room smelling Dave's burnt popcorn. So I used to go to this diner down the street from the plant. It was one of those old-school places with the shiny silver outside and the red vinyl booths. The vinyl was cracked on my favorite booth, and it would always pinch the back of my legs if I wore shorts. They had the best cherry pie, though. The waitress, Brenda, she had this big beehive hair that I swear never moved, even when the air conditioning was blasting. She told me once she used a whole can of hairspray every week. I can't imagine breathing that in every day. I tried hairspray once for my high school prom, but it just made my hair feel crunchy, like dry ramen noodles. My date was this guy named Kyle who wore a bright blue tuxedo. He looked like a giant blueberry. We took pictures by this fountain at the park, and he accidentally dropped his phone in the water. He spent the whole night complaining about it instead of dancing. But, um, yeah... the low feeling hasn't really gone away since I had to drop those classes. It's just been there every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that your movements or speech slowed down, or that you couldn’t sit still and felt restless, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've noticed anything like that. I mean, I sit pretty still at work, mostly because I have to. They give us these really uncomfortable metal stools on the line to sit on while we inspect the parts, and they don't have any padding at all. My supervisor, Carol, she has this fancy ergonomic mesh chair in her office that squeaks really loud every time she leans back. It sounds exactly like this rubber chicken my little cousin used to have. He brought it to Thanksgiving dinner a couple of years ago and kept squeezing it during the prayer, and my uncle got so mad he actually threw it over the fence into the neighbor's yard. The neighbors had this huge Rottweiler named Buster who completely shredded it in like two seconds. There was just yellow rubber everywhere in their grass. It snowed the next day, so you could just see these little bright yellow pieces sticking out of the white snow all winter. I always felt kind of bad for the dog, like what if he swallowed a piece? But um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, moving slow or being restless. I've gotten written warnings lately because I keep missing shifts, and when I am there, I'm turning in my inspection documentation late. The paperwork is just so tedious. She sent out this whole memo about only using black ink because the scanner doesn't pick up blue well enough. Which is weird, because my scanner at home picks up everything. I bought it at a garage sale for like five bucks from this guy who was moving to Florida. He had all these crazy Hawaiian shirts he was trying to sell, too. Like, neon pink with bright green palm trees. I almost bought one just as a joke, but I didn't have enough cash on me. I only had a twenty and he didn't have change. And the pump on the bottle is broken so I have to unscrew the top every single time. My apartment's water pressure is awful too, so it takes forever to rinse anything out. We actually went to this park last month, the one by the lake with the duck pond? And there were all these geese everywhere.
